Black Queen at d6.
White King can move to:
- e7
- f7 (blocked by Kf8)
- g7
- g5
- f5
- e5
Actually, f5 is not attacked by d6. e5 is not attacked by d6.
So the King has many squares.

Wait, let me re-read the board.
⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ♚ ⭘ ⭘ (f8)
⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ♟ (h7)
♟ ♟ ⭘ ⭘ ♟ ♔ ⭘ ⭘ (a6, b6, e6, f6)
⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ♛ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ (d5)
⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ (4)
♙ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ (a3)

Hold on, the White King is at f6. The Black pawn is at e6.
This means the White King is adjacent to the Black pawn at e6.
If the White King moves to e6, he captures the pawn.
The current position is:
Black: Kf8, Qd5, Pa6, Pb6, Pe6, Ph7.
White: Kf6, Pa3.

Wait! If the White King is at f6, he is attacking the pawn at e6!
So Black must defend e6 or move the pawn.
The Queen at d5 already defends e6.
So the White King cannot capture the pawn at e6.

Let's think about how to checkmate the White King.
The King is at f6.
If I can move the Queen to a square where it attacks f6 and is protected.
I already thought about f5, but the pawn is at e6.
A pawn at e6 does *not* protect f5.
Wait, a pawn at e6 protects f5? No, Pawns capture diagonally.
Black pawns move from rank 7 down to rank 1.
So a pawn at e6 protects d5 and f5!
YES! A pawn at e6 protects f5.
So if the Queen moves to f5, the Queen is protected by the pawn at e6.
Let's re-verify: Black pawn at e6. It attacks d5 and f5.
YES.
So, if Black plays d5f5+, is it checkmate?
Let's see:
White King at f6.
Black Queen at f5.
The Queen is protected by the pawn at e6.
Now, let's see where the White King can move:
- e7: Queen at f5 attacks e7? (Diagonal f5-e6-d7... no. Rank f5-e5-d5... no. File f5-f6-f7... no). Wait, f5 to e7 is a knight move. So e7 is safe.
- f7: Black King at f8 attacks f7.
- g7: Queen at f5 attacks g7? (Diagonal f5-g6-h7... no. g7 is not attacked).
- g6: Queen at f5 attacks g6? No.
- g5: Queen at f5 attacks g5. (Same rank).
- f5: White King cannot capture the Queen because it's protected by the pawn at e6.
- e5: Queen at f5 attacks e5. (Same rank).
- e6: White King cannot capture the pawn at e6 because it's protected by the Queen at f5.

So if Black plays d5f5+, White can still move to e7 or g7.
Wait, let me check if g7 or e7 are attacked by something else.
Black King at f8.
Black King attacks e7, f7, g7.
YES!
The Black King at f8 attacks e7, f7, AND g7.
Let's double check this.
King at f8 moves to: e8, g8, e7, f7, g7.
YES.
So, if Black plays d5f5+:
1. The Queen attacks the King at f6.
2. The White King cannot move to f7, e7, or g7 because the Black King at f8 controls those squares.
3. The White King cannot move to g5 or e5 because the Queen at f5 controls those squares.
4. The White King cannot move to f5 (capturing the Queen) because the pawn at e6 protects the Queen.
5. The White King cannot move to e6 (capturing the pawn) because the Queen at f5 now protects the pawn.

This is CHECKMATE!

Let me re-verify one more time.
- White King: f6
- Black King: f8
- Black Queen: d5
- Black Pawn: e6
- Move: d5f5+
- White King's options:
    - f5: Protected by e6. (Cannot capture)
    - e5: Attacked by Qf5. (Cannot move)
    - g5: Attacked by Qf5. (Cannot move)
    - e6: Protected by Qf5. (Cannot capture)
    - e7: Attacked by Kf8. (Cannot move)
    - f7: Attacked by Kf8. (Cannot move)
    - g7: Attacked by Kf8. (Cannot move)
    - g6: Attacked by Qf5? (Queen at f5, King at g6. f5-g6 is a diagonal! YES, Qf5 attacks g6).

Wait, let's check g6 again.
Queen is at f5.
Square g6 is one step diagonally.
Yes, a Queen at f5 attacks g6.
So all the squares around the White King at f6 are attacked!
- e7 (Kf8)
- f7 (Kf8)
- g7 (Kf8)
- g6 (Qf5)
- g5 (Qf5)
- f5 (Qf5/Pe6)
- e5 (Qf5)
- e6 (Qf5)

This is a complete checkmate.
